Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n  - Agitation: Excessive motor activity without obvious external cause.\n  - Grimacing: Contorted facial expressions irrelevant to the situation.\n  - Echolalia: Mimicking another's speech.\n  - Echopraxia: Mimicking another's movements.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 7 months\n▸ manic_episode\n  - Elevated Mood: A mood that is abnormally high, euphoric, and full of unmotivated happiness and vitality.\n  - Irritable Mood: A state where one gets angry or aggressive even at minor stimuli.\n  - Mood Lability: Rapid shifts in mood, alternating unpredictably between euphoria and irritability.\n  - Increased Energy: Overflowing physical and mental energy, leading to an inability to stay still and a desire to keep moving.\n  - Hyperactivity: Doing far more work than usual or starting multiple activities simultaneously.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 7 months\n▸ manic_episode\n  - Elevated Mood: A mood that is abnormally high, euphoric, and full of unmotivated happiness and vitality.\n  - Irritable Mood: A state where one gets angry or aggressive even at minor stimuli.\n  - Mood Lability: Rapid shifts in mood, alternating unpredictably between euphoria and irritability.\n  - Increased Energy: Overflowing physical and mental energy, leading to an inability to stay still and a desire to keep moving.\n  - Hyperactivity: Doing far more work than usual or starting multiple activities simultaneously.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Inability to tolerate resting or stopping, driven by a constant need to do something.\n  Duration: 3 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 1 year and 2 months\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  - Additional: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n  - Childlike Silliness: Inappropriate, childish, or illogical behavior appearing in situations.\n  - Stupor: No psychomotor activity; not actively relating to environment.\n  - Catalepsy: Passive induction of a posture held against gravity.\n  - Waxy Flexibility: Slight, even resistance to positioning by examiner.\n  - Mutism: No, or very little, verbal response despite the ability to speak.\n  - Negativism: Opposition or no response to instructions or external stimuli without apparent motive.\n  - Posturing: Spontaneous and active maintenance of a posture against gravity.\n  - Mannerism: Odd, circumstantial caricature of normal actions.\n  - Stereotypy: Repetitive, abnormally frequent, non-goal-directed movements.\n  - Agitation: Excessive motor activity without obvious external cause.\n  - Grimacing: Contorted facial expressions irrelevant to the situation.\n  - Echolalia: Mimicking another's speech.\n  - Echopraxia: Mimicking another's movements.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 7 months\n▸ manic_episode\n  - Elevated Mood: A mood that is abnormally high, euphoric, and full of unmotivated happiness and vitality.\n  - Irritable Mood: A state where one gets angry or aggressive even at minor stimuli.\n  - Mood Lability: Rapid shifts in mood, alternating unpredictably between euphoria and irritability.\n  - Increased Energy: Overflowing physical and mental energy, leading to an inability to stay still and a desire to keep moving.\n  - Hyperactivity: Doing far more work than usual or starting multiple activities simultaneously.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Inability to tolerate resting or stopping, driven by a constant need to do something.\n  Duration: 3 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - This has been going on for about 14 months now, and it is making me incredibly anxious.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your mood, such as periods of extreme happiness, irritability, or deep sadness, alongside these hallucinations and delusions?\n\nPatient: Yes, for about the past three months, I've been experiencing really intense mood changes where I feel incredibly happy and full of energy, but also extremely irritable and easily angered. These extreme shifts in my mood have definitely been happening alongside the voices and the feeling of being watched.\n\nDoctor: Did the intense mood changes and irritability start at the same time as the hallucinations and delusions, or did they begin after the psychotic symptoms were already present?\n\nPatient: The hallucinations and delusions actually started first, about seven months ago. The intense mood changes and high energy didn't begin until much later, around three months ago. So those voices and paranoid feelings were definitely already present before my mood started shifting.\n\nDoctor: \n\nPatient: I'm sorry, I didn't quite catch what you just said. Could you please repeat your last question?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of significant depression or low mood during the 14 months you've been having hallucinations and delusions, or has it primarily been the manic or high-energy episodes you mentioned?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced any periods of depression or low mood at all. My mood has strictly been high-energy, elevated, or very irritable, which has been happening right alongside the voices and paranoia over these past 14 months.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the duration and prominence of your hallucinations and delusions compared to your mood episodes - have the psychotic symptoms been present for the majority of the time, or have they been relatively brief compared to your manic or irritable episodes?\n\nPatient: The hallucinations and delusions have actually been present for the entire 14 months, including periods of at least a few weeks where my mood was completely normal. The high-energy, manic episodes have only been active for about three months of that total time, although they were very intense and prominent when they occurred.\n\nDoctor: For the majority of the 14 months, were the hallucinations and delusions the dominant symptoms, or were there periods where the mood symptoms, such as the high-energy episodes, were more prominent and the psychotic symptoms were less intense or less frequent?\n\nPatient: Actually, the high-energy mood symptoms have been present and very prominent for the majority of those 14 months. However, there was a specific period of about two or three weeks where I experienced the hallucinations and delusions entirely on their own, without any of that elevated or irritable mood.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how much of the total 14-month period you've experienced hallucinations and delusions without any significant mood symptoms, such as the high-energy or irritable episodes, and was that period continuous or intermittent?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, there was a specific continuous period of about two to three weeks where I experienced the voices and paranoia while my mood was completely normal. During those weeks, I didn't have any of the high energy, hyperactivity, or irritability. That was the only time those symptoms occurred completely on their own without the mood episodes.

However, there was a specific period of about two or three weeks where I experienced the hallucinations and delusions entirely on their own, without any of that elevated or irritable mood.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how much of the total 14-month period you've experienced hallucinations and delusions without any significant mood symptoms, such as the high-energy or irritable episodes, and was that period continuous or intermittent?\n\nPatient: Yes, there was a specific continuous period of about two to three weeks where I experienced the voices and paranoia while my mood was completely normal. During those weeks, I didn't have any of the high energy, hyperactivity, or irritability. That was the only time those symptoms occurred completely on their own without the mood episodes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the hallucinations and delusions would have occurred even if you hadn't experienced the high-energy or irritable mood episodes, or were they closely tied to the mood symptoms in your opinion?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely believe they would have occurred anyway because they started months before my mood ever changed. There were also times, like that two-to-three-week period, where I still heard the voices and felt monitored even though my mood was completely normal. They seemed to exist independently of how high or irritable my energy was.\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely believe they would have occurred anyway because they started months before my mood ever changed. Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n  - Childlike Silliness: Inappropriate, childish, or illogical behavior appearing in situations.\n  - Stupor: No psychomotor activity; not actively relating to environment.\n  - Catalepsy: Passive induction of a posture held against gravity.\n  - Waxy Flexibility: Slight, even resistance to positioning by examiner.\n  - Mutism: No, or very little, verbal response despite the ability to speak.\n  - Negativism: Opposition or no response to instructions or external stimuli without apparent motive.\n  - Posturing: Spontaneous and active maintenance of a posture against gravity.\n  - Mannerism: Odd, circumstantial caricature of normal actions.\n  - Stereotypy: Repetitive, abnormally frequent, non-goal-directed movements.\n  - Agitation: Excessive motor activity without obvious external cause.\n  - Grimacing: Contorted facial expressions irrelevant to the situation.\n  - Echolalia: Mimicking another's speech.\n  - Echopraxia: Mimicking another's movements.\n  - Diminished Emotional Expression: A state of reduced emotional expression in facial expressions, eye contact, intonation of speech, and movements.\n  - Avolition: A decrease in motivated self-initiated purposeful activities.\n  - Alogia: A state where the quantity of spontaneous speech is significantly reduced.\n  - Anhedonia: A decrease in the ability to experience pleasure or interest in enjoyable activities.\n  - Asociality: A state where interest in social activities or interpersonal relationships is significantly reduced.\n  Duration: 7 months\n▸ manic_episode\n  - Elevated Mood: A mood that is abnormally high, euphoric, and full of unmotivated happiness and vitality.\n  - Irritable Mood: A state where one gets angry or aggressive even at minor stimuli.\n  - Mood Lability: Rapid shifts in mood, alternating unpredictably between euphoria and irritability.\n  - Increased Energy: Overflowing physical and mental energy, leading to an inability to stay still and a desire to keep moving.\n  - Hyperactivity: Doing far more work than usual or starting multiple activities simultaneously.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Inability to tolerate resting or stopping, driven by a constant need to do something.\n  Duration: 3 months\n▸ delusions_and_hallucinations\n  - Persecutory Delusions: The false belief that one is being harmed or monitored by other people or organizations.\n  - Referential Delusions: The conviction that ordinary events, remarks, or behaviors have specific relevance to oneself.\n  - Grandiose Delusions: The false belief that one possesses special abilities, power, or fame.\n  - Erotomanic Delusions: The false belief that another person is in love with or has affection for them.\n  - Nihilistic Delusions: A destructive or negative conviction that the world is ending or that a major catastrophe will occur.\n  - Somatic Delusions: The baseless conviction that there is a serious abnormality with one's body or organs.\n  - Jealous Delusions: The conviction that one's spouse or romantic partner is being unfaithful without sufficient evidence.\n  - Auditory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of hearing voices or sounds when there is no actual sound.\n  - Visual Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of seeing people or objects that do not exist.\n  - Tactile Hallucinations: Experiencing the sensation of being touched or movement on the skin when there is no actual stimulus.\n  - Olfactory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of smelling odors that do not exist.\n  - Gustatory Hallucinations: The perceptual experience of tasting flavors that are not actually present.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 1 year and 2 months\n  - Additional: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  - Additional: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  - Additional: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
